GitHub Copilot: I don't have enough context to know what you want to achieve with that line. Do you want text.js to:

- Re-export everything exported by ./input?
- Re-export only a specific export from ./input (e.g., default or a named export)?
- Merge exports from multiple files into a single module?
- Convert from CommonJS to ES module syntax?
- Fix a bug such as a circular dependency or “Cannot find module” error?

Below are common patterns and alternatives — pick the one that matches your goal.

1) Re-export the entire module (CommonJS)
If you want text.js to be a simple alias that returns whatever ./input exports:
module.exports = require('./input');

2) Re-export a specific property from ./input
If ./input exports an object and you want to export only one property:
module.exports = require('./input').someExport;
(or default for transpiled ESM: .default)

3) Merge / re-export multiple modules
If you want text.js to expose a merged interface:
module.exports = Object.assign({}, require('./input'), require('./other'));

Or explicitly:
const input = require('./input');
const other = require('./other');
module.exports = {
  ...input,
  ...other
};

4) Expose both the whole module and extra properties
const input = require('./input');
module.exports = input;
module.exports.extra = require('./extra');

5) Use ES module re-exports (if package uses "type": "module" or .mjs)
export { default as input } from './input';
export * from './input';

6) Handling circular dependencies
If text and input require each other, Node returns a partially-initialized module in one of them. You can avoid problems by:
- Refactoring to remove the cycle, or
- Require inside a function (defer):
module.exports = function () {
  const input = require('./input');
  return input.doSomething();
};
or export a getter:
Object.defineProperty(module.exports, 'input', {
  get: () => require('./input')
});

7) If require('./input') fails (cannot find)
- Confirm file path: if input is in same dir, require('./input') resolves to input.js or input/index.js
- If using TypeScript or transpilation, ensure the compiled output exists at runtime.

If you tell me which goal you want (and/or paste ./input or the rest of text.js), I can provide the exact code and the minimal change needed.

bartequine-stack: // ...existing code...
module.exports = require('./input');

GitHub Copilot: That line simply re-exports whatever "./input" exports. It's a CommonJS proxy/alias — requiring "./text" elsewhere returns the exported value from "./input":

- If ./input.js does `module.exports = value`, then `require('./text')` returns that same value.
- If ./input is a transpiled ES module (`{ default: x }` shape), `require('./text')` returns that object (so you'd access `require('./text').default`).

Common usage and alternatives

- Basic proxy (your current snippet)
  module.exports = require('./input');

- Make sure to re-export the default if the target may be an ES module:
  module.exports = require('./input').default || require('./input');

- Re-export named properties or merge modules:
  module.exports = Object.assign({}, require('./input'), require('./other'));

- With ES modules (if you are using ESM syntax):
  export { default } from './input';  // re-export default
  export * from './input';           // re-export named exports

Troubleshooting

- If you get "Cannot find module './input'", verify the relative path and that the file exists (Node will try .js, .json, .node extensions).
- If `require('./text')` returns an object with `.default`, you likely have ESModule/CJS interop; use the `.default ||` pattern above if you want the raw default exported value.
- If you rely on live bindings (ESM-style), CommonJS `require` copies/returns the export object; for most uses that’s fine.

If you tell me what you want to achieve (e.g., re-export default only, export named exports, fix a runtime error), I can suggest an exact change.

GitHub Copilot: I’m not sure exactly what you want changed or what error you’re getting, but here are the most common problems and fixes for a line like:

module.exports = require('./Iinput');

Possible issues and solutions

1) Typo / case mismatch
- If the actual file is named input.js, Input.js, or iInput.js, change the require to match exactly (Linux is case-sensitive):
  - module.exports = require('./input');
  - module.exports = require('./Input');

2) File doesn’t exist at the path
- If the path is wrong, ensure the relative path is correct: use ../ to go up from the current folder.
  - module.exports = require('../Iinput'); // example if in parent folder

3) ES module default export vs CommonJS
- If ./Iinput is an ES module compiled to CJS with a default export (e.g., Babel), require('./Iinput') may return an object with { default: ... }.
  - module.exports = require('./Iinput').default || require('./Iinput');

4) Named exports
- If ./Iinput exports named members and you want to forward them:
  - module.exports = require('./Iinput'); // forwards the whole exports object
  - or export a subset:
    - const { foo, bar } = require('./Iinput');
      module.exports = { foo, bar };

5) Using ES module syntax (if you’re using ESM)
- Replace CommonJS line with:
  - export { default } from './Iinput';
  - or export * from './Iinput';

6) Circular dependency (rare)
- If require('./Iinput') requires back the file that contains this line, that can cause undefined exports. Refactor to avoid the circular require, or require lazily inside functions where needed.

Debugging tips
- Run a quick check to see what Node resolves:
  - console.log(require.resolve('./Iinput'));
- Wrap require in try/catch to see the actual error stack:
  - try { module.exports = require('./Iinput'); } catch (e) { console.error(e); }

If you share the exact error message you’re seeing (or the filename that should be required), I can give a specific fix.

# AI Toolkit — Tools Manifest
Version: 1.0.0
Generated: 2025-12-13
Maintainer: AI Toolkit Team

Purpose
- Single comprehensive manifest describing all available AITK tools, expected inputs/outputs, example invocations, best practices and troubleshooting notes.
- Intended for engineers, evaluators and integrators using the toolkit during AI/Agent development and evaluation.

Table of Contents
- aitk-get_agent_code_gen_best_practices
- aitk-get_tracing_code_gen_best_practices
- aitk-get_ai_model_guidance
- aitk-evaluation_planner
- aitk-get_evaluation_code_gen_best_practices
- aitk-evaluation_agent_runner_best_practices
- Global guidelines
- Contribution & change log

------------------------------------------------------------
aitk-get_agent_code_gen_best_practices
Short description
- Provides recommended patterns, architecture, and practical steps for designing and generating agent code.

Detailed description
- Covers agent architecture patterns (reactive, deliberative, hybrid), modular composition (perception, reasoning, action), integration guidelines for LLMs and external tools, data pipelines, training vs inference planning, security and safety considerations, observability.

Inputs
- agentType (reactive|deliberative|hybrid)
- language (js|ts|py|java)
- applicationDomain (chat|assistant|automation|control)
- targetPlatform (server|edge|browser|mobile)
- constraints (latency, memory, cost)
- optional: examples of conversation, test dataset

Outputs
- Architectural recommendation document (markdown)
- Minimal scaffold/boilerplate code snippet for agent runtime
- Suggested evaluation metrics specific to agent type

Usage examples
- aitk-get_agent_code_gen_best_practices --agentType reactive --language ts --applicationDomain chat --targetPlatform server
- JSON/CLI output supports piping to files: aitk-get_agent_code_gen_best_practices ... > agent-design.md

Best practices (key takeaways)
- Separate concerns: perception, policy/reasoning, action, and state persistence.
- Keep LLM dependency encapsulated behind an interface for easier change.
- Consider multi-turn context management strategies: token budget, chunking, memory store.
- Use policies and guardrails for safety: acceptability filters, anonymization, rate limits.
- Implement proper instrumentation early (metrics, traces, logs).
- Provide feature flags and canary deployments for iterative rollout.

Pitfalls & troubleshooting
- Overrelying on LLM outputs as authoritative — always validate outputs for critical actions.
- Missing or insufficient test harness for iterative changes — unit tests and simulation runners are essential.

------------------------------------------------------------
aitk-get_tracing_code_gen_best_practices
Short description
- Guidelines and automated support for generating tracing/instrumentation code aligned with best practices for AI applications.

Detailed description
- Focus on observability: distributed tracing, structured logging, metrics, privacy-aware tracing, and end-to-end correlation for agents and LLM calls.

Inputs
- platform (otel|aws-xray|gcp-stackdriver|custom)
- tracesSampleRate (0..1)
- privacySensitivity (low|medium|high)
- retentionDays
- languages (js|py|go|java)
- example endpoints or functions to instrument

Outputs
- Instrumentation code snippets and templates
- Config file for tracing provider with sampling & retention
- Example dashboards and alert rules

Usage examples
- aitk-get_tracing_code_gen_best_practices --platform otel --languages js,py --tracesSampleRate 0.1
- Generates recommended config for OpenTelemetry + snippet to instrument LLM calls

Best practices
- Sample and aggregate: don't trace everything at 100% in production; sample but keep metadata to reconstruct root causes.
- Redact or anonymize PII before logging/tracing to meet privacy rules.
- Correlate traces with unique request/agent IDs for end-to-end debugging.
- Track LLM prompt/response size and token usage as custom metrics.
- Ensure low overhead in hot paths — asynchronous log/trace shipping helps.

Pitfalls & troubleshooting
- Exposing secrets or user sensitive content in traces; always sanitize.
- Unbounded cardinality on tags leads to metrics explosion; avoid high-cardinality user IDs unless hashed.

------------------------------------------------------------
aitk-get_ai_model_guidance
Short description
- Advice and decision guides for selecting models, prompt engineering, and optimization for cost/latency/accuracy.

Detailed description
- Compares model families, selection heuristics for latency/cost/accuracy, guidance for prompt design and few-shot strategies, caching/policy ideas, and fallback designs.

Inputs
- useCase (summarization|qa|generation|classification|chat)
- modelFamily (gpt|llama|bloom|custom)
- constraints (tokenBudget|latency|cost)
- performanceGoals (accuracy/recall/f1/bleu)
- requiredCertifications (if any)

Outputs
- Shortlist of models and rationale
- Prompt templates and guardrail snippets
- Recommendations for caching and safety checks

Usage examples
- aitk-get_ai_model_guidance --useCase qa --modelFamily gpt --constraints "latency:200ms;cost:0.0001"

Best practices
- Align model choice with both technical constraints and evaluation metrics.
- Provide simple, robust fallbacks and deterministic heuristics (e.g., retrieval + ranking pipelines).
- Use prompt templates and store them centrally for maintenance.
- Leverage model chaining only when it is clearly beneficial.
- Track token utilization and latency in production.

Pitfalls & troubleshooting
- Picking a model purely on benchmark results without considering real-world inputs.
- Not testing prompts on diverse, noisy user examples.

------------------------------------------------------------
aitk-evaluation_planner
Short description
- Interactive planner to define evaluation metrics, datasets, and evaluation strategy by guiding the user through a structured conversation.

Detailed description
- Guides teams through clarifying objectives: evaluation type (automated, human-in-the-loop, A/B), data sample creation, metrics selection, baseline definition, and variance estimation.

Inputs
- domain (chat|qa|code|image)
- metricFocus (accuracy|helpfulness|safety|cost|latency)
- existingDataSources (urls/path)
- evaluationMode (automated|human|mixed)

Outputs
- Evaluation plan (markdown) that includes dataset splits, metrics, baseline, evaluation timeline and roles
- Test harness specification and scoring rubrics
- Recommended annotation guidelines for human raters

Usage examples
- aitk-evaluation_planner --domain chat --metricFocus "safety,helpfulness" --evaluationMode mixed
- Returns an evaluation plan and an example CSV schema for dataset.

Best practices
- Define clear acceptance thresholds and primary vs secondary metrics.
- Include thorough instructions and anchor examples for human raters.
- Validate inter-rater reliability before main annotation run.
- Use stratified sampling to cover edgecases and worst-case behavior.
- Automate as much as possible (pre-processing, score aggregation) to avoid introduction of errors.

Pitfalls & troubleshooting
- Ambiguous metrics (e.g., “helpful” without a rubric).
- Not capturing distribution shifts in the test dataset.

------------------------------------------------------------
aitk-get_evaluation_code_gen_best_practices
Short description
- Best practices to generate and structure evaluation code, test harnesses, automation, and reproducibility strategies.

Detailed description
- Template code, structured benchmark harnesses, reproducibility (seeded runs), CI integration, and performance/tracing instrumentation for evaluation.

Inputs
- language (py|js|ts)
- runnerType (local|k8s|serverless)
- metrics (list)
- datasetSchema (CSV|JSONL)
- seed (optional)
- parallelism

Outputs
- Example harness (CLI/Code sample) to run evaluation
- Standard metric reporting schema & example artifacts
- CI job examples and lightweight reproducible environment config

Usage examples
- aitk-get_evaluation_code_gen_best_practices --language py --runnerType local --metrics "accuracy,latency"

Best practices
- Ensure deterministic runs: version control data and model references; use seeded shuffles.
- Save raw outputs for reruns and audits.
- Use a clear metrics JSON schema for downstream aggregation.
- Avoid manual steps: integrate in CI for nightly/regression tests.
- Store artifacts and results centrally for reproducibility and audits.

Pitfalls & troubleshooting
- Non-deterministic evaluation due to unstated seeding or parallel race conditions.
- Using different pre-processing or tokenization between training/inference/evaluation.

------------------------------------------------------------
aitk-evaluation_agent_runner_best_practices
Short description
- Practical advice for running agent-based evaluation workloads (parallelization, error handling, result aggregation, and rate limiting).

Detailed description
- Runner architectures for agent evaluation (single-process, WSGI, containerized batch), scheduling, retry/backoff, partial failures and continuing runs, collection of logs/traces and summaries.

Inputs
- concurrency (int)
- retryPolicy (none|exponential|fixed)
- rateLimit (requests/s)
- outputFormat (jsonl|csv)
- resultsS3/path (url)
- sampleSize

Outputs
- Runner scaffolding code and runner config files
- Example CLI to launch evaluation jobs
- Template for aggregated results and error reporting dashboard

Usage examples
- aitk-evaluation_agent_runner_best_practices --concurrency 10 --retryPolicy exponential --outputFormat jsonl --resultsS3 s3://my-bucket/evals

Best practices
- Use idempotent run steps and an explicit checkpointing mechanism for long evaluations.
- Enforce backoff and respect rate limits for external dependencies (APIs, LLM endpoints).
- Create health-checks and watchdogs to detect hung runs.
- Aggregate results in a central store and provide standard export formats.
- Tag each run with a run_id, timestamp, and git commit hash for traceability.

Pitfalls & troubleshooting
- Failing on partial errors with insufficient logging — always capture response payloads and stack traces for failed cases.
- Overparallelizing leading to noisy results or API throttling.

------------------------------------------------------------
Global Guidelines
- Config Management:
  - Keep tool config in version control (YAML/JSON) and follow configuration drift prevention.
- Security & Privacy:
  - Redact PII from traces and logs by default.
  - Follow organizational encryption at rest & in transit policies.
- Testing & CI:
  - Automate evaluation runs and gating with CI; run smoke tests for every PR.
- Monitoring & Alerts:
  - Configure alerts for regressions on primary metrics and error rate spikes.
- Naming & Metadata:
  - All evaluation and agent runs should include run_id, commit sha, environment, and a reproducible config snippet.
- Documentation:
  - Keep prompt templates and evaluation rubrics versioned and in the repo.

Additions & Contribution Guide
- To add a new tool or update the manifest:
  1. Add a new section in tools.md-manifest following the same structure (short desc, inputs, outputs, usage, best practices, pitfalls).
  2. Provide at least one usage example and a sample config if applicable.
  3. Run a quick smoke test or include a script that verifies the generated code snippets compile or run.
  4. Submit PR with clear changelog entry.

Change Log
- v1.0.0 — 2025-12-13 — Initial manifest generated with full entries for listed tools.

------------------------------------------------------------
Appendix: Example "how to run" an agent generation + evaluation flow (CLI)
- Step 1: Generate agent architecture
  - aitk-get_agent_code_gen_best_practices --agentType hybrid --language ts --applicationDomain chat > agent-design.md
- Step 2: Decide model and tracing
  - aitk-get_ai_model_guidance --useCase chat --modelFamily gpt
  - aitk-get_tracing_code_gen_best_practices --platform otel --languages ts --tracesSampleRate 0.1
- Step 3: Create evaluation plan
  - aitk-evaluation_planner --domain chat --metricFocus "helpfulness,safety" --evaluationMode mixed > eval-plan.md
- Step 4: Generate evaluation scaffolding
  - aitk-get_evaluation_code_gen_best_practices --language py --runnerType local --metrics "accuracy,latency" > eval-harness.py
- Step 5: Run evaluation job, collect results and iterate
  - aitk-evaluation_agent_runner_best_practices --concurrency 16 --retryPolicy exponential --outputFormat jsonl --resultsS3 s3://my-bucket/evals

End of manifest

GitHub Copilot: I don't have enough context to know what you want to achieve with that line. Do you want text.js to:

- Re-export everything exported by ./input?
- Re-export only a specific export from ./input (e.g., default or a named export)?
- Merge exports from multiple files into a single module?
- Convert from CommonJS to ES module syntax?
- Fix a bug such as a circular dependency or “Cannot find module” error?

Below are common patterns and alternatives — pick the one that matches your goal.

1) Re-export the entire module (CommonJS)
If you want text.js to be a simple alias that returns whatever ./input exports:
module.exports = require('./input');

2) Re-export a specific property from ./input
If ./input exports an object and you want to export only one property:
module.exports = require('./input').someExport;
(or default for transpiled ESM: .default)

3) Merge / re-export multiple modules
If you want text.js to expose a merged interface:
module.exports = Object.assign({}, require('./input'), require('./other'));

Or explicitly:
const input = require('./input');
const other = require('./other');
module.exports = {
  ...input,
  ...other
};

4) Expose both the whole module and extra properties
const input = require('./input');
module.exports = input;
module.exports.extra = require('./extra');

5) Use ES module re-exports (if package uses "type": "module" or .mjs)
export { default as input } from './input';
export * from './input';

6) Handling circular dependencies
If text and input require each other, Node returns a partially-initialized module in one of them. You can avoid problems by:
- Refactoring to remove the cycle, or
- Require inside a function (defer):
module.exports = function () {
  const input = require('./input');
  return input.doSomething();
};
or export a getter:
Object.defineProperty(module.exports, 'input', {
  get: () => require('./input')
});

7) If require('./input') fails (cannot find)
- Confirm file path: if input is in same dir, require('./input') resolves to input.js or input/index.js
- If using TypeScript or transpilation, ensure the compiled output exists at runtime.

If you tell me which goal you want (and/or paste ./input or the rest of text.js), I can provide the exact code and the minimal change needed.

GitHub Copilot: That line simply re-exports whatever "./input" exports. It's a CommonJS proxy/alias — requiring "./text" elsewhere returns the exported value from "./input":

- If ./input.js does `module.exports = value`, then `require('./text')` returns that same value.
- If ./input is a transpiled ES module (`{ default: x }` shape), `require('./text')` returns that object (so you'd access `require('./text').default`).

Common usage and alternatives

- Basic proxy (your current snippet)
  module.exports = require('./input');

- Make sure to re-export the default if the target may be an ES module:
  module.exports = require('./input').default || require('./input');

- Re-export named properties or merge modules:
  module.exports = Object.assign({}, require('./input'), require('./other'));

- With ES modules (if you are using ESM syntax):
  export { default } from './input';  // re-export default
  export * from './input';           // re-export named exports

Troubleshooting

- If you get "Cannot find module './input'", verify the relative path and that the file exists (Node will try .js, .json, .node extensions).
- If `require('./text')` returns an object with `.default`, you likely have ESModule/CJS interop; use the `.default ||` pattern above if you want the raw default exported value.
- If you rely on live bindings (ESM-style), CommonJS `require` copies/returns the export object; for most uses that’s fine.

  err.message = `Failed to load ./input for enquirer text prompt: ${err.message}`;
  throw err;
}
// ...existing code...

bartequine-stack: update and make a new big tools.md-manifest with contents

GitHub Copilot: ````markdown
# AI Toolkit — Tools Manifest
Version: 1.0.0
Generated: 2025-12-13
Maintainer: AI Toolkit Team

Purpose
- Single comprehensive manifest describing all available AITK tools, expected inputs/outputs, example invocations, best practices and troubleshooting notes.
- Intended for engineers, evaluators and integrators using the toolkit during AI/Agent development and evaluation.

Table of Contents
- aitk-get_agent_code_gen_best_practices
- aitk-get_tracing_code_gen_best_practices
- aitk-get_ai_model_guidance
- aitk-evaluation_planner
- aitk-get_evaluation_code_gen_best_practices
- aitk-evaluation_agent_runner_best_practices
- Global guidelines
- Contribution & change log

------------------------------------------------------------
aitk-get_agent_code_gen_best_practices
Short description
- Provides recommended patterns, architecture, and practical steps for designing and generating agent code.

Detailed description
- Covers agent architecture patterns (reactive, deliberative, hybrid), modular composition (perception, reasoning, action), integration guidelines for LLMs and external tools, data pipelines, training vs inference planning, security and safety considerations, observability.

Inputs
- agentType (reactive|deliberative|hybrid)
- language (js|ts|py|java)
- applicationDomain (chat|assistant|automation|control)
- targetPlatform (server|edge|browser|mobile)
- constraints (latency, memory, cost)
- optional: examples of conversation, test dataset

Outputs
- Architectural recommendation document (markdown)
- Minimal scaffold/boilerplate code snippet for agent runtime
- Suggested evaluation metrics specific to agent type

Usage examples
- aitk-get_agent_code_gen_best_practices --agentType reactive --language ts --applicationDomain chat --targetPlatform server
- JSON/CLI output supports piping to files: aitk-get_agent_code_gen_best_practices ... > agent-design.md

Best practices (key takeaways)
- Separate concerns: perception, policy/reasoning, action, and state persistence.
- Keep LLM dependency encapsulated behind an interface for easier change.
- Consider multi-turn context management strategies: token budget, chunking, memory store.
- Use policies and guardrails for safety: acceptability filters, anonymization, rate limits.
- Implement proper instrumentation early (metrics, traces, logs).
- Provide feature flags and canary deployments for iterative rollout.

Pitfalls & troubleshooting
- Overrelying on LLM outputs as authoritative — always validate outputs for critical actions.
- Missing or insufficient test harness for iterative changes — unit tests and simulation runners are essential.

------------------------------------------------------------
aitk-get_tracing_code_gen_best_practices
Short description
- Guidelines and automated support for generating tracing/instrumentation code aligned with best practices for AI applications.

Detailed description
- Focus on observability: distributed tracing, structured logging, metrics, privacy-aware tracing, and end-to-end correlation for agents and LLM calls.

Inputs
- platform (otel|aws-xray|gcp-stackdriver|custom)
- tracesSampleRate (0..1)
- privacySensitivity (low|medium|high)
- retentionDays
- languages (js|py|go|java)
- example endpoints or functions to instrument

Outputs
- Instrumentation code snippets and templates
- Config file for tracing provider with sampling & retention
- Example dashboards and alert rules

Usage examples
- aitk-get_tracing_code_gen_best_practices --platform otel --languages js,py --tracesSampleRate 0.1
- Generates recommended config for OpenTelemetry + snippet to instrument LLM calls

Best practices
- Sample and aggregate: don't trace everything at 100% in production; sample but keep metadata to reconstruct root causes.
- Redact or anonymize PII before logging/tracing to meet privacy rules.
- Correlate traces with unique request/agent IDs for end-to-end debugging.
- Track LLM prompt/response size and token usage as custom metrics.
- Ensure low overhead in hot paths — asynchronous log/trace shipping helps.

Pitfalls & troubleshooting
- Exposing secrets or user sensitive content in traces; always sanitize.
- Unbounded cardinality on tags leads to metrics explosion; avoid high-cardinality user IDs unless hashed.

------------------------------------------------------------
aitk-get_ai_model_guidance
Short description
- Advice and decision guides for selecting models, prompt engineering, and optimization for cost/latency/accuracy.

Detailed description
- Compares model families, selection heuristics for latency/cost/accuracy, guidance for prompt design and few-shot strategies, caching/policy ideas, and fallback designs.

Inputs
- useCase (summarization|qa|generation|classification|chat)
- modelFamily (gpt|llama|bloom|custom)
- constraints (tokenBudget|latency|cost)
- performanceGoals (accuracy/recall/f1/bleu)
- requiredCertifications (if any)

Outputs
- Shortlist of models and rationale
- Prompt templates and guardrail snippets
- Recommendations for caching and safety checks

Usage examples
- aitk-get_ai_model_guidance --useCase qa --modelFamily gpt --constraints "latency:200ms;cost:0.0001"

Best practices
- Align model choice with both technical constraints and evaluation metrics.
- Provide simple, robust fallbacks and deterministic heuristics (e.g., retrieval + ranking pipelines).
- Use prompt templates and store them centrally for maintenance.
- Leverage model chaining only when it is clearly beneficial.
- Track token utilization and latency in production.

Pitfalls & troubleshooting
- Picking a model purely on benchmark results without considering real-world inputs.
- Not testing prompts on diverse, noisy user examples.

------------------------------------------------------------
aitk-evaluation_planner
Short description
- Interactive planner to define evaluation metrics, datasets, and evaluation strategy by guiding the user through a structured conversation.

Detailed description
- Guides teams through clarifying objectives: evaluation type (automated, human-in-the-loop, A/B), data sample creation, metrics selection, baseline definition, and variance estimation.

Inputs
- domain (chat|qa|code|image)
- metricFocus (accuracy|helpfulness|safety|cost|latency)
- existingDataSources (urls/path)
- evaluationMode (automated|human|mixed)

Outputs
- Evaluation plan (markdown) that includes dataset splits, metrics, baseline, evaluation timeline and roles
- Test harness specification and scoring rubrics
- Recommended annotation guidelines for human raters

Usage examples
- aitk-evaluation_planner --domain chat --metricFocus "safety,helpfulness" --evaluationMode mixed
- Returns an evaluation plan and an example CSV schema for dataset.

Best practices
- Define clear acceptance thresholds and primary vs secondary metrics.
- Include thorough instructions and anchor examples for human raters.
- Validate inter-rater reliability before main annotation run.
- Use stratified sampling to cover edgecases and worst-case behavior.
- Automate as much as possible (pre-processing, score aggregation) to avoid introduction of errors.

Pitfalls & troubleshooting
- Ambiguous metrics (e.g., “helpful” without a rubric).
- Not capturing distribution shifts in the test dataset.

------------------------------------------------------------
aitk-get_evaluation_code_gen_best_practices
Short description
- Best practices to generate and structure evaluation code, test harnesses, automation, and reproducibility strategies.

Detailed description
- Template code, structured benchmark harnesses, reproducibility (seeded runs), CI integration, and performance/tracing instrumentation for evaluation.

Inputs
- language (py|js|ts)
- runnerType (local|k8s|serverless)
- metrics (list)
- datasetSchema (CSV|JSONL)
- seed (optional)
- parallelism

Outputs
- Example harness (CLI/Code sample) to run evaluation
- Standard metric reporting schema & example artifacts
- CI job examples and lightweight reproducible environment config

Usage examples
- aitk-get_evaluation_code_gen_best_practices --language py --runnerType local --metrics "accuracy,latency"

Best practices
- Ensure deterministic runs: version control data and model references; use seeded shuffles.
- Save raw outputs for reruns and audits.
- Use a clear metrics JSON schema for downstream aggregation.
- Avoid manual steps: integrate in CI for nightly/regression tests.
- Store artifacts and results centrally for reproducibility and audits.

Pitfalls & troubleshooting
- Non-deterministic evaluation due to unstated seeding or parallel race conditions.
- Using different pre-processing or tokenization between training/inference/evaluation.

------------------------------------------------------------
aitk-evaluation_agent_runner_best_practices
Short description
- Practical advice for running agent-based evaluation workloads (parallelization, error handling, result aggregation, and rate limiting).

Detailed description
- Runner architectures for agent evaluation (single-process, WSGI, containerized batch), scheduling, retry/backoff, partial failures and continuing runs, collection of logs/traces and summaries.

Inputs
- concurrency (int)
- retryPolicy (none|exponential|fixed)
- rateLimit (requests/s)
- outputFormat (jsonl|csv)
- resultsS3/path (url)
- sampleSize

Outputs
- Runner scaffolding code and runner config files
- Example CLI to launch evaluation jobs
- Template for aggregated results and error reporting dashboard

Usage examples
- aitk-evaluation_agent_runner_best_practices --concurrency 10 --retryPolicy exponential --outputFormat jsonl --resultsS3 s3://my-bucket/evals

Best practices
- Use idempotent run steps and an explicit checkpointing mechanism for long evaluations.
- Enforce backoff and respect rate limits for external dependencies (APIs, LLM endpoints).
- Create health-checks and watchdogs to detect hung runs.
- Aggregate results in a central store and provide standard export formats.
- Tag each run with a run_id, timestamp, and git commit hash for traceability.

Pitfalls & troubleshooting
- Failing on partial errors with insufficient logging — always capture response payloads and stack traces for failed cases.
- Overparallelizing leading to noisy results or API throttling.

------------------------------------------------------------
Global Guidelines
- Config Management:
  - Keep tool config in version control (YAML/JSON) and follow configuration drift prevention.
- Security & Privacy:
  - Redact PII from traces and logs by default.
  - Follow organizational encryption at rest & in transit policies.
- Testing & CI:
  - Automate evaluation runs and gating with CI; run smoke tests for every PR.
- Monitoring & Alerts:
  - Configure alerts for regressions on primary metrics and error rate spikes.
- Naming & Metadata:
  - All evaluation and agent runs should include run_id, commit sha, environment, and a reproducible config snippet.
- Documentation:
  - Keep prompt templates and evaluation rubrics versioned and in the repo.

Additions & Contribution Guide
- To add a new tool or update the manifest:
  1. Add a new section in tools.md-manifest following the same structure (short desc, inputs, outputs, usage, best practices, pitfalls).
  2. Provide at least one usage example and a sample config if applicable.
  3. Run a quick smoke test or include a script that verifies the generated code snippets compile or run.
  4. Submit PR with clear changelog entry.

Change Log
- v1.0.0 — 2025-12-13 — Initial manifest generated with full entries for listed tools.

------------------------------------------------------------
Appendix: Example "how to run" an agent generation + evaluation flow (CLI)
- Step 1: Generate agent architecture
  - aitk-get_agent_code_gen_best_practices --agentType hybrid --language ts --applicationDomain chat > agent-design.md
- Step 2: Decide model and tracing
  - aitk-get_ai_model_guidance --useCase chat --modelFamily gpt
  - aitk-get_tracing_code_gen_best_practices --platform otel --languages ts --tracesSampleRate 0.1
- Step 3: Create evaluation plan
  - aitk-evaluation_planner --domain chat --metricFocus "helpfulness,safety" --evaluationMode mixed > eval-plan.md
- Step 4: Generate evaluation scaffolding
  - aitk-get_evaluation_code_gen_best_practices --language py --runnerType local --metrics "accuracy,latency" > eval-harness.py
- Step 5: Run evaluation job, collect results and iterate
  - aitk-evaluation_agent_runner_best_practices --concurrency 16 --retryPolicy exponential --outputFormat jsonl --resultsS3 s3://my-bucket/evals

End of manifest
````
